Sinceμ2=−μ1\mu_{2}=-\mu_{1},(θ^1−θ^2)′=μ1​(W1−1+W2−1)>0.(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\widehat{\theta}_{2})^{\prime}=\mu_{1}(W_{1}^{-1}+W_{2}^{-1})>0.

Thusθ^1−θ^2\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\widehat{\theta}_{2}increases from0to32​θ^1​(Th)>π/2\tfrac{3}{2}\widehat{\theta}_{1}(T_{\mathrm{h}})>\pi/2.
The intermediate value theorem gives a uniquet∗∈(0,Th)t_{*}\in(0,T_{\mathrm{h}})withθ^1​(t∗)−θ^2​(t∗)=π2.\widehat{\theta}_{1}(t_{*})-\widehat{\theta}_{2}(t_{*})=\frac{\pi}{2}.(C.1)

Thust∗t_{*}and−t∗-t_{*}lie on opposite branches with the same
magnitude,
and the sign changeσ↦−σ\sigma\mapsto-\sigmain the circle factor
converts their relative block-phase difference into a common scalar
phase.

Reflection across the lower turn givess​(−t)=s​(t)s(-t)=s(t)andθ^i​(−t)=−θ^i​(t)\widehat{\theta}_{i}(-t)=-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(t).
Forσ∈𝕊1\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{1}, setz−=Yγ​(t∗,σ),z+=Yγ​(−t∗,−σ).z_{-}=Y_{\gamma}(t_{*},\sigma),\qquad z_{+}=Y_{\gamma}(-t_{*},-\sigma).

Chooseϑ∗∈ℝ\vartheta_{*}\in\mathbb{R}so thatei​ϑ∗=e−2​i​θ^1​(t∗)e^{i\vartheta_{*}}=e^{-2i\widehat{\theta}_{1}(t_{*})}.
Direct substitution in
(C.1)
givesz+=ei​ϑ∗​z−z_{+}=e^{i\vartheta_{*}}z_{-}.
On the outgoing branch,Lemma˜5.8givesθ^1​(t∗)+2​θ^2​(t∗)=−arctan⁡D−1,C2​(s​(t∗))∈(−π/2,0).\widehat{\theta}_{1}(t_{*})+2\widehat{\theta}_{2}(t_{*})=-\arctan\sqrt{D_{-1,C_{2}}(s(t_{*}))}\in(-\pi/2,0).

Sinceθ^1​(t∗)−θ^2​(t∗)=π/2\widehat{\theta}_{1}(t_{*})-\widehat{\theta}_{2}(t_{*})=\pi/2,e3​i​ϑ∗=e−2​i​(θ^1​(t∗)+2​θ^2​(t∗))∉{1,−1}.e^{3i\vartheta_{*}}=e^{-2i(\widehat{\theta}_{1}(t_{*})+2\widehat{\theta}_{2}(t_{*}))}\notin\{1,-1\}.

Everyζ∈𝖧L\zeta\in\mathsf{H}_{L}satisfiesζ3=±1\zeta^{3}=\pm 1, by comparison of the oriented
special-Lagrangian cone volume under scalar multiplication.
Henceei​ϑ∗∉𝖧Le^{i\vartheta_{*}}\notin\mathsf{H}_{L}:
the incidence is pointwise but not global,
soLLis not phase-saturated.

Substitution in the outer map givesF​(0,z+,z−)=F​(ϑ∗,z−,z+)F(0,z_{+},z_{-})=F(\vartheta_{*},z_{-},z_{+}).
If the two tangent spaces agreed, projection to the first complex
block and the Legendrian condition would implyTz+​L=ei​ϑ∗​Tz−​LT_{z_{+}}L=e^{i\vartheta_{*}}T_{z_{-}}L.
Adjoining the radial directions and comparing cone phases would then
forcee3​i​ϑ∗=±1e^{3i\vartheta_{*}}=\pm 1, a contradiction.
Hence the two branches have distinct tangent spaces.
The outer profile12​(ei​ϑ,e−i​ϑ)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\vartheta},e^{-i\vartheta})is steady, minimal, and contact;
hence the outer product is minimal Legendrian and becomes special
Legendrian after the constant phase rotation used in the definition ofFF.
∎

## C.3.Projective-domain cover and factor-Hessian identities

This subsection uses the phase-saturated canonical lifts and the
quotient-closed projective domains ofSection˜6.3.
Setp=n1+1p=n_{1}+1,q=n2+1q=n_{2}+1, andm=p+qm=p+q.
Write𝒳γ=Xγ/𝖧Gγ\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}=X_{\gamma}/\mathsf{H}_{G_{\gamma}}and letpi:L^i→Lip_{i}:\widehat{L}_{i}\to L_{i}be the canonical horizontal covers.
ByTheorem˜6.5(ii),
scalar identifications preserve branch type and translate the profile
by multiples ofNNcomplete cells.
For every seam or scalar identification,
the residual phase in theii-th block lies in𝖧fi\mathsf{H}_{f_{i}};
henceRemark˜6.2identifies its action onL^i\widehat{L}_{i}with the corresponding
canonical deck transformation.
Therefore round arclength modulo the total lengthTTof theseNNcells and the maps𝔭i​([τ,x^1,x^2])=pi​(x^i),i=1,2,\mathfrak{p}_{i}([\tau,\widehat{x}_{1},\widehat{x}_{2}])=p_{i}(\widehat{x}_{i}),\qquad i=1,2,

descend to𝒳γ\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}.
The map𝔓:=(τ,𝔭1,𝔭2):𝒳γ⟶𝕊T1×L1×L2\mathfrak{P}:=(\tau,\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}):\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}^{1}_{T}\times L_{1}\times L_{2}

is a local diffeomorphism and satisfies𝔓∗​gw=g𝒳γ\mathfrak{P}^{*}g_{\mathrm{w}}=g_{\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}},
withgwg_{\mathrm{w}}given by
(6.3).
Compactness of the domain and connectedness of the target make𝔓\mathfrak{P}a finite surjective Riemannian covering.

The product deck group𝖧f1×𝖧f2\mathsf{H}_{f_{1}}\times\mathsf{H}_{f_{2}}acts on𝒳γ\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}, commutes with the seam and scalar
identifications, and acts transitively on every fiber of𝔓\mathfrak{P}. Its kernel isdiag⁡(𝖣12)={(k,k):k∈𝖣12}\operatorname{diag}(\mathsf{D}_{12})=\{(k,k):k\in\mathsf{D}_{12}\}, because
exactly these fixed-time factor actions are common scalar symmetries.
Thus𝔓\mathfrak{P}is regular, and the ratio map inducesDeck⁡(𝔓)≅𝖧f1×𝖧f2diag⁡(𝖣12)≅𝖪12,deg⁡(𝔓)=ν1​ν2|𝖣12|=κ.\operatorname{Deck}(\mathfrak{P})\cong\frac{\mathsf{H}_{f_{1}}\times\mathsf{H}_{f_{2}}}{\operatorname{diag}(\mathsf{D}_{12})}\cong\mathsf{K}_{12},\qquad\deg(\mathfrak{P})=\frac{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}{|\mathsf{D}_{12}|}=\kappa.

For this finite cover,
transfer satisfiestr∘𝔓∗=κ​Id\operatorname{tr}\circ\mathfrak{P}^{*}=\kappa\,\operatorname{Id}on real cohomology.
Thus𝔓∗\mathfrak{P}^{*}is injective,
and the Künneth formula gives
(6.4).

On this warped cover, writeϖ=𝔞n1/2​𝔟n2/2\varpi=\mathfrak{a}^{n_{1}/2}\mathfrak{b}^{n_{2}/2}for the profile part of the volume density.
LetΔ0\Delta_{0}be the positive Laplacian on𝒳γ\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}, and letΔ0,i\Delta_{0,i}be the positive
Laplacian on(Li,gi)(L_{i},g_{i}).
For brevity, write𝔞1:=𝔞=cos2⁡s\mathfrak{a}_{1}:=\mathfrak{a}=\cos^{2}sand𝔞2:=𝔟=sin2⁡s\mathfrak{a}_{2}:=\mathfrak{b}=\sin^{2}sfor the squared block norms,r1=pr_{1}=p, andr2=qr_{2}=q.

Here𝔞i>0\mathfrak{a}_{i}>0on the projective domain;
since𝔭i\mathfrak{p}_{i}is surjective, each𝒯i\mathcal{T}_{i}is injective.

(6.6)
givesΔ0​𝔞1=2​m​𝔞1−2​p\Delta_{0}\mathfrak{a}_{1}=2m\mathfrak{a}_{1}-2p.
Since𝔞2=1−𝔞1\mathfrak{a}_{2}=1-\mathfrak{a}_{1}andq=m−pq=m-p,

Hence𝒯i​ϕ\mathcal{T}_{i}\phihas mean zero wheneverϕ\phidoes.
Oh’s bilinear Hamiltonian form isQL¯γHam​(ϕ,ψ)=∫(Δ0​ϕ​Δ0​ψ−2​m​⟨∇ϕ,∇ψ⟩)​d​vol.Q_{\overline{L}_{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{Ham}}(\phi,\psi)=\int\bigl(\Delta_{0}\phi\,\Delta_{0}\psi-2m\langle\nabla\phi,\nabla\psi\rangle\bigr)\,d\operatorname{vol}.

the preceding operator identity gives, fori=1,2i=1,2,QL¯γHam​(𝒯i​ϕ,𝒯i​ψ)=𝔠i​∫Li(Δ0,i−2​ri)​ϕ​Δ0,i​ψ​d​volLi=𝔠i​QℓiHam​(ϕ,ψ),Q_{\overline{L}_{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{Ham}}(\mathcal{T}_{i}\phi,\mathcal{T}_{i}\psi)=\mathfrak{c}_{i}\int_{L_{i}}(\Delta_{0,i}-2r_{i})\phi\,\Delta_{0,i}\psi\,\,d\operatorname{vol}_{L_{i}}=\mathfrak{c}_{i}Q_{\ell_{i}}^{\mathrm{Ham}}(\phi,\psi),

where𝔠1=κ​Vol⁡(L2)​I0\mathfrak{c}_{1}=\kappa\operatorname{Vol}(L_{2})I_{0}and𝔠2=κ​Vol⁡(L1)​I0\mathfrak{c}_{2}=\kappa\operatorname{Vol}(L_{1})I_{0}, so𝔠i>0\mathfrak{c}_{i}>0.
For every mean-zero input potentialϕ\phi,∫Li(Δ0,i−2​ri)​ϕ​d​volLi=−2​ri​∫Liϕ​d​volLi=0.\int_{L_{i}}(\Delta_{0,i}-2r_{i})\phi\,\,d\operatorname{vol}_{L_{i}}=-2r_{i}\int_{L_{i}}\phi\,\,d\operatorname{vol}_{L_{i}}=0.

Fubini’s theorem therefore shows that the mixed factor terms and
their pairings with profile-only potentials vanish.
Consequently, the two transferred negative factor spaces and any
negative profile-only space form aQL¯γHamQ_{\overline{L}_{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{Ham}}-orthogonal direct sum.
This provesLemma˜6.10and the factor-sector identities used inTheorem˜6.11.
